1316. JAMES ASTON & CORNELIUS GLANDFIELD were indicted for stealing, on the 25th of May, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of a man unknown, from his person.
CHARLES GEORGE VINCENT. I am a constable. On Sunday week, about half-past nine o'clock in the evening, I was in the City-road; I saw a person, who I believe was Glandfield, take a handkerchief from a gentleman's pocket, and give it to Aston, who put it under the tail of his coat, and was crossing the road, I went up to him, and took him - I put my hand down by his side, where I thought I had seen the handkerchief and endeavoured to take it from him; I felt his hand move, but did not see the handkerchief go from him; Mr. Hall came up, and asked if I had lost anything - I said I had not, but another person had picked a gentleman's pocket of his handkerchief, and given it to the prisoner; he said he had seen something thrown away, and ran and got this handkerchief - I believe Glandfield was the personwho took it; I went the next morning, and took him in Chequer-place.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Was it not rather dark? A. Yes, but there was a light opposite me - a person named Toole was with me - I ordered Toole to go after the gentleman.
THOMAS TOOLE. I was with Vincent; I told him I saw the two prisoners in company trying pockets - we followed them, and saw them go towards a gentleman; Glandfield took the handkerchief from him, and gave it to Aston; I said, "They have got it" - he crossed the road, and took Aston; I was going after Glandfield, but he said, Go, tell the gentleman - I went, but could not overtake him; I knew where Glandfield lived, and told Vincent.
WILLIAM HALL. I am inspector of the watch. I was standing at the Star public-house; I saw Vincent collar Aston; and while he had hold of him, I saw Aston throw the handkerchief down - I asked Vincent if he had lost anything; he said Aston had received a handkerchief; I told him to hold him fast - I went to the place where the handkerchief fell; a little boy took it up and gave it to me.
TIMOTHY DONOVAN. I picked up this handkerchief; I did not see who threw it down.
ASTON'S Defence. There were two or three men in the road; the officer collared me and took me.
GLANDFIELD'S Defence. I was in bed at half past eight o'clock.
ASTON - GUILTY. Aged 17.
GLANDFIELD - GUILTY. Aged 14.
Confined Three Months and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18280529-238]
1317. SOPHIA FITZPATRICK was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of May, 3 sovereigns, the monies of John Butcher, from his person.
JOHN BUTCHER. I am a watchman. On Thursday last I was in Bagnigge-wells-Road, between five and six o'clock in the afternoon; the prisoner came up to me, took hold of my arm, put her hand in my coat pocket and took out three sovereigns, rolled up in a dirty piece of cloth - I felt her hand in my pocket - I put my hand into my pocket and missed my money - I followed her into the Wells, but she had turned a corner and I missed her; I came out, went round and went in again; I then met her and charged her with robbing me of three sovereigns; I gave her in charge of Barnes, who took one sovereign from her hand; an officer took her into a room and found two more sovereigns on her; she had laid hold of my arm - my money was safe just before I met her.
JOSEPH HENRY BARNES. I am an appraiser and was at Bagnigge-wells. Butcher said he had lost a sovereign; I took hold of the prisoner's hand; some persons came up and wanted to let her go; I saw her hand behind her, took hold of it, and took the sovereign out of it; the officer came up and found the other two sovereigns and a piece of rag; she said "It makes no odds to me whether he gave me a sovereign or a shilling." I said "Where are the other two?" She said she had no more.
AUSTIN HARLE. I am an officer. I was sent for - the prisoner said nothing to the charge; I found two sovereigns and a dirty rag on her.
JOHN BUTCHER. This is like my piece of rag.
Prisoners' Defence. He asked me to accompany him to the gardens, and when there he asked me to go with him, and he would give me three shillings; he gave me this and I found it was three sovereigns.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18280529-239]
1318. EDWARD COOKE was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of May, 9 lbs. weight of horse-hair, value 35s.; 1 coat, value 10s.; 2 pairs of pantaloons, value 17s.; 1 waistcoat, value 18d.; and 1 handkerchief, value 1d. the goods of John Salter.
JOSEPH LLOYD. I am a watchman. I met the prisoner on Sunday evening, the 4th of May, about ten o'clock, at the top of East-street, Hoxton, about two hundred yards from Mr. Salter's with a bundle - I asked what was in it, he said some washing; I took it from under his arm, and seeing some hair and the rest of these articles in it, I took him to the watch-house.
JOHN SALTER. I am a horse-hair manufacturer, and live at Hoxton. On Sunday, the 4th of May, I went out at four o'clock in the afternoon, leaving my door on the single lock; I came home about eleven o'clock at night - I had drunk rather more than had done me good; the door was locked - I went in and went up to bed - my wife perceived the things disturbed; we had left nobody at home; we looked and missed these articles from the first-floor front room.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Had you been drinking before you left home? A. I had taken a little - I know I pulled the door too.
CHARLES CONSTABLE. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house; I searched him and found some picklock keys, a crow-bar, and phosphorus box on him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had been drinking - I heard a cry, ran out to see what it was, and these things lay in the road - I took them up.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280529-240]
1319. JOHN HALE and JOHN KENT were indicted for stealing, on the 8th of May, 6 bushels of oats and chaff mixed, value 8s., the goods of Hugh McIntosh, their master; and WILLIAM HAYNES was indicted for feloniously receiving the same, knowing them to have been stolen.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
WILLIAM BANNISTER. I am clerk to Hugh McIntosh, who is employed at the New Palace - Hale and Kent drove his team of horses.
THOMAS HINDER. I am in Hugh McIntosh's employ. I had to drive the horses from St. James's-park to Richmond, to fetch turf to beautify the gardens; the ostler puts the horse corn out in sacks every morning at the stable-yard at Westminster; I was employed for eight days going to Richmond, till the 14th of May - we had about three bushels of chaff and corn every morning to feed fourteen horses. On the 8th of May Hale and Kent gave a sack of corn to Haynes, at the Old George public-house, Brompton; he gave him all that the ostler had given out for the horses; Haynes was at the stable - I saw him give Hale half-a-crown, and Kent told me hehad another; I should think it was worth about 5s.; Mr. McIntosh's name was on the cart - Haynes took the corn into the stable and stowed it; he returned the sack directly, and they went away; when Kent heard that Mr. McIntosh had heard of it, he came to me and said he would give me three sovereigns if I would say nothing about it - I told of it last Saturday week, not before.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you mention the three sovereigns at Bow-street? A. Yes - I do not know whether my evidence was read over to me; I believe it was; I did not see what was in the sack - it was given out to us as corn; I abused Kent, because the corn was not given to the horses - I had no money; they offered me beer several times, but I refused it - I had one pint with them; I did not like to make a disturbance about it, as I had only been there a few days - I had no quarrel with them about a jacket, it was with another servant; I did not mention it till after that - we had the nose-bag full for the horses to eat.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Had you any quarrel with them? A. No; the horses had nothing more than was in the corn-bin - Colopy was with me.
JOHN COLOPY. I was employed to drive a team for Mr. McIntosh. I saw Hale and Kent give Haynes two sacks of corn, at the George, at Brompton; he took it into the stable, and brought the sacks out again.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you see the sacks filled? A. Yes - 5s. 6d each would be a fair price for them; I knew it was for the horses - I did not like to tell master, but I did three days after; I only see the foreman once a week - I had part of the beer which was bought with the money - I cannot say how much I drank; they were taken up about a week after.
THOMAS POWELL. I am ostler at Mr. McIntosh's stable. I gave out two sacks, each containing three bushels, every day for the horses; and did so on the 8th of May - it was chaff and corn mixed; Mr. McIntosh's name was on the cart.
Cross-examined. Q. How long did the men remain in the employ? A. I do not know - I knew nothing of it till master told me.
RICHARD STARKEY. I know this corn - this mixture is worth 4s. or 5s. each sack.
KENT'S Defence. The witness is a false man - he lost a jacket, and said if we did not give it up he would go and tell of the robbery which he never saw.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280529-241]

[trialAccount t18280529-243]
1322. DOMINICK FEELEY was indicted for bigamy.
MR. CLARKSON conducted the prosecution.
SARAH SOUTH. I am a widow, and live on Saffron-hill. The prisoner lived with me - he was married to Jane Cowley; they lived together at my house - they went out to be married and slept at my house after that; she went by his name, and they lived together for five or six months - the prisoner asked me to drink with him and his wife; I am sure he called her his wife.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Was it not a Mrs. Hyde you saw? A. No - her name was Cowley; she is outside the Court now; they left me to keep a public-house, but I have not seen her for ten or twelve years till lately.
JOSEPH MOULD. I am parish-clerk of St. Andrew, Holborn. I have the register of the marriage of Jane Cowley and Thomas Feeley, by licence, on the 22d of February, 1812.
CHARLES FISHER SAVIGNEY. I am beadle of Holborn, and know Jane Feeley. In November, 1812, I apprehended the prisoner for deserting his wife; she applied for relief to the parish - I relieved her; she is now at the door - the prisoner acknowledged her as his wife, and was committed to prison.
Cross-examined. Q. Have you seen much of his wife since? A. No - not till latterly.
SARAH GRIFFITHS. I am a widow - the prisoner was clerk to my late husband, who kept the Cooper's Arms, public-house, Crown-street, Finsbury. I was married to the prisoner at Shoreditch Church on Shrove Tuesday; he passed as a single man for some time before we were married.
Cross-examined. Q. I believe you and your husband did not live on the best of terms? A. Why I found he had another wife, but I would not appear against him; he died six years ago - I made him support me, for he had 2000l. of money; I have not been reluctant to prosecute the prisoner - I never lived with a Mr. Chippendale.
Q. How long did you live with the prisoner before you married him? A. That is not a fair question; I did not live with him; he paid me a sovereign a week.
Q. Did you not cohabit with him? A. Why as many of the first ladies in the land have done I did do it; I knew he had a wife; when I found that out we parted beds, but lived together for about a month - I discovered it six weeks ago; I have no children - he had no money with me. I have been arrested - the prisoner owes me nearly 200l.; I keep the Coopers' Arms now; the license is not in the prisoner's name.
GEORGE YARROW. I am clerk of Shoreditch; I have a register of the prisoner's marriage to S. Griffiths on the 19th of February, 1828. - (read.)
Prisoner's Defence. This is a complete conspiracy against me - this woman will swear though a nine inch board - she owes me 100l. now.
GUILTY. Aged 34.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280529-244]
1323. JOHN MACINTOSH was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of May, 1 box, value 6s.; 4 coats, value 7l.; 5 pairs of trousers, value 2l.; 10 shirts, value 50s.; 12 pairs of stockings, value 24s.; 12 handkerchiefs, value 1l., and 8 waistcoats, value 1l., the goods of James Mackenzie.
JAMES MACKENZIE. I am a ladies' shoemaker. Icame to town on Tuesday, and left my box on board the smack at Hoares' wharf, in care of the steward; when I returned on Thursday, one box, containing these articles was gone; some persons on board gave me information - Crighton took us to a house in Rosemary-lane, where the prisoner lived; the people were out - I went for a constable, he came and found the box in the back room; it had been broken open, and several things taken out.
DAVID CRIGHTON. I am a porter, and work at Hoares' wharf. I carried a yellow trunk by the prisoner's order, from the vessel to the White Swan public-house, Rosemary-lane; he went there with me - he said it was his own - I was on the wharf waiting for a job; I am sure he said it was his own, and he said he was a passenger in that vessel, the Forfairshire; I was called again at eight o'clock that night, and went with the prosecutor to find it.
JOSEPH KING. I am a porter. The prisoner applied to me on Thursday afternoon last, to carry a box from the White Swan; I took it to a house in Brown's-buildings, Princes street, and left it there - he said it was his box. I afterwards went with the prosecutor, who claimed the same box - there was a rope round it, and it appeared locked.
SARAH HAWKINS. I live in Brown's-buildings; the prisoner was a friend of my husband's; he called on Thursday, and asked me to let him leave this box in my place, which I did; the prosecutor came and took it.
JOHN FORRESTER. I am an officer. I found the box in Brown's-buildings, with some articles in it.
CHARLES WALLER. I apprehended the prisoner in Rosemary-lane, and found a shirt, trousers, coat, and waistcoat, on him - he was dressed in them, and his own coat was left in the box, which had been broken open with a chisel or hatchet.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I expected a box from Scotland; I had been drinking rather freely, and thought this was mine - the linen is marked as my own.
GUILTY. Aged 52.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280529-245]
First London Jury - before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1324. HENRY GREENLEY was indicted for feloniously breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Maxwell Windle, Esq. on the 4th of January, at the Middle Temple, and stealing therein, 1 cape of a great coat, value 6d.; 6 pairs of trousers, value 36s.; 5 coats, value 30s.; 6 waistcoats, value 6s.; 1 great coat, value 5s.; 3 handkerchiefs, value 6s.; 2 pairs of gloves, value 6d.; 1 pair of socks, value 3s.; 1 wooden box, value 1s.; 1 syringe, value 5s.; 1 tea-caddy, value 6s.; 2 tea-ladles, value 4s.; 1 tea-spoon, value 4s., and 1 canvass-bag, value 1s., his property.
MR. EVANS conducted the prosecution.
MARY BARKER. I am laundress to Mr. Windle. I locked up his chambers, which are in the Middle Temple, on Friday the 4th of January; he had gone out of town a few days before - I had gone there every day, except Sunday; I went there again on the 5th of January, about four o'clock in the afternoon - I found the outer door only on one lock, and the other door open, the blinds were drawn down, and the lamp was on the floor; it was in a very different state to what I had left it on the 4th - I looked round and was rather frightened, but did not know whether Mr. Windle had been there or not; I locked the chambers and went away - I went again on the next day, which was Sunday, a little after three o'clock, to look more particularly - the first thing I then saw was one of the drawers of the office table was broken open; I looked farther, and found all the drawers were broken open but two, which had not been locked, and the cellaret was broken open; I missed a tea-caddy, a brown silk umbrella, a clothes-bag, and a key, which were left at the chambers; I have since seen the caddy in Mr. Windle's possession, and the clothes-bag at Worship-street - I found a crow-bar and a chisel in the chambers; I gave them to the porter, and a gentleman who I fetched to the chambers- the outer door had not been broken; the key went in as easily as when I left it, but was only locked once - I had locked it twice.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Are you positive of that? A. Yes; I remarked, as I did not mean to go again that day that I had locked it safe - I found it singly locked on the Saturday; I double locked it on the Friday - I found the cupboards and drawers open, and the foul linen on the floor; I did not examine minutely that day, being alarmed and thinking Mr. Windle might have been there unknown to me - I made no more inquiry that day; I noticed it more on Sunday, when I went there to see if I could find Mr. Windle; I did not look for the key on Saturday - I locked the door when I came out; I went on Sunday to Mr. Tattersal's in Bloomsbury-square, and told him of it - I never saw the prisoner till he was taken up some months after; I heard he had been taken and was liberated - I have been a laundress four or five years.
MR. EVANS. Q. Did you send to Mr. Tattersal on the Saturday? A. Yes, but he was not at his chambers; I went to his house on Sunday, and found him - he is a friend of Mr. Windle's; I attend to three other gentlemen's chambers.
ROBERT LOCK. I am a constable. On Friday the 4th of January, between five and six o'clock in the afternoon, I was going up Whitecross-street, and saw the prisoner crossing the road; he crossed Whitecross-street, went up a turning, and knocked at a backdoor; I followed him, as he seemed in a hurry, and passed him two or three yards - I then saw him turn back, and go into Whitecross-street again; I lost sight of him for about a minute and a half. I then saw him come across, and go into the front door of a house with this bag - I followed him in directly, and asked what he had got there; he said his father's clothes - I said that would not satisfy me, he must go with me to the watch-house: I took him there, and then said, "How did you say you got these things?" He said, "I met a gentleman in Holborn, who employed me to carry this bag:" I asked if he knew the gentleman; he said, "No:" I said, "Do you know what is in the bag;" he said No; I said "I must know;" I looked in and found all these articles - I have kept most of them ever since, but not all; I searched him and found these keys on him, and thinking one was not a right key, I said I should detain him - I looked into his hat and found some handkerchiefs; he was taken to Worship-street and examined three times, and then discharged as no owner was found.
Cross-examined. Q. Was he held to bail? A. No; hewas at full liberty - he knew he was discharged, because I could not find the owner; he was taken again in three or four months - he was at liberty till then; he knew he was liable to be taken if the owner was found.
MR. EVANS. Q. Do you recollect when you found the owner? A. I think about a week after he was discharged - the prosecutor came down to me; I then searched for him many a night and day.
MAXWELL WINDLE, ESQ. These articles are all my property; I was out of town on the 4th of January; I left them in my chamber in the Middle Temple, where I reside when in town: I rent the chambers - they are entirely my own - I sleep there.
JAMES BROWN. I am an officer of Worship-street. I apprehended the prisoner in a room in George-street, Shoreditch, on the 9th of May, about half-past six o'clock in the morning: I told him it was for Mr. Windle's robbery; he said, "It is of no use - I know I must go with you;" I had seen him the night before, in the skittle-ground of a public-house in Plough-yard, Shoreditch, but he made his escape. I found two skeleton-keys in his room.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you call these skeleton-keys? A. Yes - they are skeleton-keys for Bramah's locks; I got them from a young woman who was in bed with him. I have been an officer nearly fifteen years.
Prisoner's Defence. I was coming down Holborn on the 4th of January; a gentleman came and asked me to carry this bundle, and said he would give me a shilling; I followed him down Whitecross-street, when I was shoved into a shop by Lock, and taken; I had four hearings, and was discharged.
Three witnesses gave the prisoner a good character.
GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 21. (See page 618.)
[trialAccount t18280529-246]

1326. THOMAS LEWIS STYLES was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 gold neck-chain, value 6l., the goods of Thomas Dobson and others, his masters, in the dwelling-house of the said Thomas Dobson.
HENRY GLOVER. The prisoner has been shopman to Mr. Thomas Dobson, and others, jewellers, of Leadenhall-street, for about seven weeks. On the 11th of April, as I went to a back door, I picked up a small coral necklace; I made strict inquiry about it of the prisoner and every person in the house, but could get no information; in about an hour the prisoner came to me, and said, "Mr. Glover, I am sorry anybody should be blamed for my errors, as it respects that coral necklace; I dropped it myself:" he said he had a commission from his mother to get a necklace of that description for his sister and he had taken it to show her, and on Monday he should return it, or pay the money; I said it was a thing he had no right to do, and sent for Mr. Dobson into the warehouse; I then said Styles had said he had dropped the necklace, and that he wished to show it to his mother; I should have said he was about to leave us; we thought it prudent to have him searched, and sent for an officer: when the officer came, he put his hand into his pocket, and pulled out a variety of articles of his employer's property: he delivered up the key of his trunk, in which were found other articles mentioned in another indictment; a duplicate of the gold neck-chain was found in a pocket-book, on his person - he said it was our property.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. All he said was, that it was your's? A. Yes, I am one of the partners.
THOMAS EMBERSON. I am a pawnbroker, and have a gold neck-chain, pawned by the prisoner, on the 7th of April, for 4l. - that is nearly the value.
GEORGE FITCH. I took him in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Of stealing to the value of 80s. only.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
There was another indictment against the prisoner.

1327. ISAAC GRAVES was indicted for feloniously assaulting Nathaniel Jerrom, on the 18th of May, putting him in fear and taking from his person and against his will 2 half-crowns, his property.
NATHANIEL JERROM. I am a tailor. On Saturday morning the 18th of May, between twelve and one o'clock, I was coming home from the Borough, and in Bishopsgate-street I saw Mitchell sitting on a step; I helped her up, and she asked me to give her a glass of gin; I said it was late; she took me to where she knew that she could get it; but before I could get into the house the prisoner seized me by the right arm and Mitchell had hold of my left; put his hand into my right-hand pocket, and took out two half-crowns; my hat fell off; I got up and tried to escape; he struck me a violent blow on the back of my head; it cut my head - I am certain of his person; he stood by me for two minutes; he was not apprehended till Sunday night.
JOHN BROWN. Between twelve and one o'clock in the morning of Saturday, the 18th of May, the prosecutor came home and described the prisoner; I knew him well by the description, and told him to come to me in the evening - I took him to the Black Raven, public-house, Bishopsgate-street, and asked if he could point out the man; he pointed to the prisoner and said, "This is the man I am certain."
SARAH MITCHELL. I met the prosecutor; I had hold of his left arm when the prisoner turned his right-hand pocket inside out - I saw him do it, but did not see him take any thing; I am certain of his person; it was not him who struck him.
Prisoner's Defence. I was in Angel-alley and heard a cry of Watch! I turned back and heard a gentleman had been robbed of two half-crowns - I went straight home.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Of stealing from the person only. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18280529-249]
1328. JOHN WESTON and JAMES WESTON were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of April, 1 phial of Cayenne-pepper, value 1s., and 1 pot of tamarinds, value 6d., the goods of William Dadds, and another.
WILLIAM DADDS. I am a grocer, and live in Leadenhall-street, in partnership with Mr. Eding. On the 27th of April, about eleven o'clock in the morning, I was in the counting-house, Stevens came in and said, our window was cut - I ran out; Stevens said, "Follow me;" I called two men - we pursued the prisoners andtook them; we found this property on John Weston, about eighteen doors from my house; it was safe, and the glass secure half an hour before.
JOHN STEVENS. About eleven o'clock on the 27th of April, I saw the prisoners near the window; I saw Jones with a knife cutting the putty - I crossed over, and immediately they broke the window; James cut the window - John was close to him, holding a bag over his arm - I went and told Mr. Dadds, who came out, and we took them; I am certain of their persons - they were taken in five minutes; I never lost sight of them.
THOMAS GREY. I am an officer. I found this bottle and pot of tamarinds in John's pocket, and this knife with putty on it; the putty appeared quite fresh cut - I found on James a sack.
JOHN WESTON'S Defence. I was standing above a yard from him, the knife was not out of my pocket, I passed the window and picked these things up.
JOHN WESTON - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
JAMES WESTON - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Six Months.

1329. WILLIAM MILLS was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of April, 2 yards of twilled cotton, value 1s., the goods of John Clark, his master.
MR. PHILLIPS conducted the prosecution.
DANIEL FORRESTER. I am an officer. On the evening of the 22d of April, I watched Mr. Clark's warehouse, and saw the prisoner come out; I followed him down East Smithfield, where he joined a woman, and went into a liquor-shop; I followed him, pulled off his hat, and found these two pieces of calico - he said, "You don't mean to take me for this, we will have a drop of something to drink; you don't know me, nor I you;" I took him to his master's counting-house, and he said, he took it to make a pair of drawers - he said this quite voluntarily.
ROBERT BYFIELD. I am in Mr. John Clark's employ. The prisoner was brought back in custody; I did not see him until next morning - I saw the goods safe at four o'clock.(Property produced and sworn to.)
HENRY PHILLIPS. I am in Mr. Clark's employ, and was present when the prisoner was brought back; no threat or inducement was held out to him - he said, "It is only a piece of brown cotton, which I took to make myself a pair of drawers;" the officer said, "Then you acknowledge you had it from Mr. Clark's?" he said, Yes; he has been in our employ one year and three quarters.
GUILTY. Aged 49.
Strongly recommended to Mercy. - Confined Two Months.

1330. WILLIAM BENTLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of May, 1 pelisse, value 6s., and 1 shawl, value 4s., the goods of Stephen Sandford.
STEPHEN SANDFORD. I keep an eating-house in Duke-street. I missed a shawl and a pelisse from my bed-room, on the 3d of May - I suspected the prisoner, and found him on the 6th of May, in custody; I did not know him before.
JOHN LAWS. I was on duty in Newgate-street on the 5th of May; the prosecutor came and gave the prisoner into my charge.
NATHANIEL ROUTH. I was at Guildhall when the prisoner was brought there; he was discharged, to be sent on board a ship - as I went along with him, I said, "It is a pity the man should lose his property;" he then cried, and said, he would show me where it was - he took me to the pawnbroker's; and said, he did not intend to go away without letting the man know where it was.
HENRY WHARTON. I am a pawnbroker. On the 1st of May the prisoner pawned this pelisse, and on the 26th of April, this shawl.
Prisoner. It was all through starvation.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.

1331. PETER SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of May, 2 looking-glasses, value 5s., the goods of Arthur Wilcoxon, and others, his masters.
ARTHUR WILCOXON. I am a looking-glass manufacturer, and live in Lombard-street; the prisoner was in my employ for about twelve months - on the 24th of May I lost these two glasses.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. You sell a great number? A. Yes; I cannot say I have not sold them; I had a nine years character with him - I have two partners.
FRANCIS KEYS. On the 24th of May I was watching Mr. Wilcoxon's porters out; I saw the prisoner come out, and followed him, he went into a pawnbrokers in Houndsditch, and pawned these glasses - I then followed him to Southwark-bridge and took him.
THOMAS CAPPS. I am a pawnbroker. The prisoner pawned these glasses.
JAMES FOWLER. I went to the prisoner's lodgings and found another glass.

1332. WILLIAM AUSTIN and RICHARD SAFFERY were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of May, 1 gallon of porter, value 1s. 2d., the goods of Henry Hole, in a certain barge on the navigable river Thames .
HENRY HOLE. I am a barge owner. On the 11th of May I had some porter on board to go to Canterbury - the prisoners were employed on board.
SAMUEL BLYTH. I am a Thames Police-surveyor. On the 11th of May, at 2 o'clock in the morning, I saw a light on board the Brigg barge, and saw a can handed down from the deck; I went on board and found Saffery on the deck; I said "What have you there?" He said,"Porter and a general cargo;" I went into the hold and found a spile in the porter cask - I said, "You have been spiling a cask, and I saw a can handed down;" Austin, who was below, said it was necessary to spile casks - I searched for the can, and said it was very odd I could not find it; I went down a second time with Dean, the master, and found the can under a sack, with a quantity of porter in it - I found a gimblet on Austin, and a piece of a different spile; the gimblet was wet, andfitted the hole in the cask; Austin said, "We were very dry, and thought no harm of taking a little porter."
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Does not the beer work at times? A. Yes; they then make a hole in the top of the cask, but this was in the head; their work is hard.
COURT. Q. Were they at work there? A. No.
WILLIAM CARTER WESTCOTT. I am a cooper and examined the cask; there was a hole in the bilge and a spile in the head, where we never allow a spile to be stuck; it was not in that state when it left Messrs. Trueman and Co., my employers.
WILLIAM BELL. I live at Messrs. Hanbury's, and filled the casks; there was no spile in them.
THOMAS MORRIS. I was with Blyth; I saw Saffery hand the cask into the hold; I am certain he was the man: I was not above two yards from him; Austin was below.
HENRY HOLE. They were hard-working men; I am so convinced of their honesty that I will take them into my employ again.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280529-254]
1333. CHARLES BURFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of April, 1 watch, value 3l.; 2 seals, value 18d.; 1 watch-key, value 6d.; 1 ring, value 6d.; and 1 piece of ribbon, value 1d., the goods of John Storment, from his person.

1334. MARY ANN DONOVAN was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of May, 3 sovereigns, 12 shillings and 4 sixpences, the monies of Jonathan Wilkinson, from his person.
JONATHAN WILKINSON. I live near Uxbridge. On Tuesday last I was in Fleet-market with Brown; he asked me to have a glass of something; we were going to Mr. Mason's, and met the prisoner with another young woman; the prisoner asked if we would give her anything to drink; we gave her no answer; she followed us; my friend gave me a glass of gin and the prisoner one: she then asked me for one; I made her no answer - she pushed close up to me as I turned to speak to Mr. Mason, and then my purse was gone from my pocket; I said to her immediately, "You have robbed me - you have picked my pocket and got my purse and money;" she declared she had not; I said I would have her searched; I sent for an officer, and just as he came I saw the purse drop down at the counter; there was no one within a yard of her; I took it up, but the money was gone; she was taken to the watch-house; I have not recovered the money; it was all safe half an hour before, when I gave some change.
JOEL BROWN. I was with Wilkinson at half-past six o'clock in the morning; we went into the wine-vaults; he said he had lost his purse and money - the prisoner was at his side and the other young woman was close to him; they were close to him for about ten minutes; I saw the prisoner put her hand into her bosom, take out the purse, and pass it outside her linen, between her thighs; she kept it there about three minutes and then let it fall; the other woman, who was close to her, turned round, and asked if I would accept of a glass of spirits; I refused and placed myself against the door; there were seven or eight men there; some of them went out at the other-door.
BENJAMIN KIRBY. I am a patrol. Wilkinson gave the prisoner and another woman into my charge; I saw the purse at the prisoner's feet, and at the Compter we found 8s.6d. on her; she was very violent and saucy, and singing.
RICE PRICE. I took her in charge; here is the purse and 8s.6d.(Purse produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. We were going to a coffee-shop; the prosecutor was selling water-cresses, and said he would treat us; he poured out a glass of gin and peppermint; he was drunk and said he had lost his money; he accused the other woman of it and then me; I never saw his purse till it was on the floor.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280529-256]
1335. SAMUEL BARNETT was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of April, one order for payment of and value 2l., the property of John Dangerfield.
JOHN BIGGERSTAFF. I live in Smithfield, and am a money-dealer. On the 15th of April, the prisoner presented a cheque for 2l. at my office - the payment had been stopped; I asked him how he got it - he said he found it at Temple-bar the night preceding - I gave him in charge.
JOHN DANGERFIELD. This cheque is payable to me - it was in my possession on the 14th of April; I took it about eight o'clock that morning, of Mr. Rhodes, the drawer, in Wellington-street, Strand; I had it safe about eleven o'clock, when I was on Tower-hill - I was at Limehouse-bridge between nine and ten o'clock at night, and was knocked down and robbed of my hat and this cheque - there might be four or five men who knocked me down; the cheque was in my breeches pocket - I had been drinking.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Were you not drunk? A. No - nor was I sober; I could have walked home - I live at East Ham; I cannot say that I ever saw the prisoner - I was not in Fleet-street that day - Yes, I was certainly, but I did not drink till I got to Tower-hill: I went to two public-houses; I drank nothing but gin and beer - I was on Tower-hill about ten o'clock in the morning; I cannot tell what houses I went into, or at what times I drank - I was not at four public-houses; I went and stopped the cheque next morning - I was knocked down by the side of the river.
COURT. Q. Between eleven o'clock in the morning and nine at night, did you see your cheque safe? A. Yes; between four and five - I walked home that night.
Prisoner's Defence. As I was going to Temple-bar to my uncle's, I found a piece of paper; I kept it till next morning and asked the gentleman if it was payable.
JOHN BENNETT. I am a master butcher and live at No. 10 1/2, Petticoat-lane, next door to the prisoner's parents. On Monday evening, the 14th of April I saw him at home, from five o'clock till eight; his house is about two miles from Limehouse-bridge - I saw him in the shop standing at the counter all the time.
ALFRED WOOLF. I am a corn-merchant. I know nothing of the prisoner, but serve his brother with flour; I saw him in his brother's shop on the 14th of April, from seven till eight o'clock at night.
NOT GUILTY.

SIXTH DAY. WEDNESDAY, JUNE 4.
Second London Jury - before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1336. CORNELIUS MARTIN was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of April, 1 handkerchief, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Charles Harrington Twight, from his person.
CHARLES HARRINGTON TWIGHT. On the 16th of April, between seven and eight in the evening, I was in Smithfield inquiring my way and felt my handkerchief drawn from my pocket; I turned round, and a woman said"A man has drawn your handkerchief from your pocket;" I saw the prisoner running; I pursued and cried Stop thief! the patrol stopped him - I had not lost sight of him; he was about five or six yards from me when I first saw him; there were some persons by his side, but none between us - he ran straight forward; a little boy picked up the handkerchief - it is the one I lost.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Were you able to describe the handkerchief at the watch-house? A. Yes; I was not positive whether there were any initials on it - I said it was like one I had at home.
WILLIAM SPENCER. I am a patrol. I saw the prisoner stopped - the prosecutor was running after him; a lad gave me the handkerchief.
JOHN SPRATMAN. I saw the prisoner running and saw him drop the handkerchief; I took it up and gave it to the officer.
GEORGE GODFREY. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house - the prosecutor said he thought there was some yellow in the colour of the handkerchief.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280529-258]
1337. JAMES MORLAND was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of May, 1 coat, value 3l.; 1 waistcoat, value 20s.; 1 petticoat, value 6s.; 2 frocks, value 6s.; 1 apron, value 6d.; 1 other petticoat, value 1s., and 1 shawl, value 8s., the goods of Thomas Jennings.
ANN SOPHIA JENNINGS. I am the wife of Thomas Jennings. On the 6th of May I saw this property safe in a box in my room about eight o'clock in the morning; I saw the prisoner at the bottom of the court at ten - I saw him again in my room at half-past eleven o'clock, getting out of my window; I was going into my room, and he pushed against the door - I waited some time, and saw him go from behind the door and stand in the middle of the room- I cried out, "Lord have mercy upon me;" Stephens came from the water-closet, and the prisoner made his way out at the window; I went in and shut the window, put the fastenings on it, and sent Stephens to tell my husband; I went and fetched Mr. Worrall to go into my room with me; I found my trunk broken open, and the articles stated taken from it; I saw the prisoner in about three hours.
JOHN STEVENS. I was at Mr. Jennings'; I saw the prisoner climbing up at the window; I did not see whether he got out - I went to call Mr. Jennings.
GEORGE HAZLEWOOD WORRALL. I am an officer. - I went to the house, and missed the things from the trunk in the lower room; I found this chisel just inside the door - we looked round, and found all the articles in this sack behind the door.
GEORGE GODFREY. I heard of the robbery, and took the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I was an out-patient of St. Bartholomew-hospital; I went and got some pills there the day I was taken; I then went to Mrs. Elbury's and staid there from eleven o'clock in the morning till two in the afternoon; I then went to a public-house, and had a pint of half and half - Godfrey came in and said I was his prisoner - I went to Guildhall, and the woman first swore to David Sullivan; she then described me, and swore to me.
G. H. WORRALL. She said there was another person with him named Sullivan.
JOHN STEVENS. I did not see any other person.
MARIA ELBURY. I keep a green-grocer's shop at No. 15, Peter-street. I have known the prisoner about eight months - he lived in an up stairs room of mine about eight months ago; he has borne a good character. On the day he was taken up, he came to my house to ask me if the landlord had a room to let; I said Yes, and he said he would wait for the landlord; he waited from ten minutes past eleven o'clock till near two, and then went away, and I never saw him any more; I do not know what day of the week it was, but it was the beginning of May, about the 5th or 6th - I am quite sure it was the day he was taken up, because his father came the day after, to ask me if he had taken a room in the house - I am quite certain he did not move from the step of my door till two o'clock that day.
JURY to A. S. JENNINGS. Q. What time did you see him in the court where you live? A. From ten o'clock till past eleven; I keep a stand in the court.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280529-259]
1338. JOHN FARRELL was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of May, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of William Taylor, from his person.
WILLIAM TAYLOR. On Thursday last, about one o'clock in the day, I was near Holborn-hill; I felt some one pulling at my pocket - I turned round, but did not observe anybody: a little boy told me that the prisoner had taken my handkerchief; he was then playing on the pavement with money; I challenged him with it - he denied it, and said if I would let go of his collar he would walk with me; I let him go; he ran from me - a gentleman stopped him.
JAMES HARVEY DAVIES. I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief, and give it to another boy, who ran down Field-lane with it - it was a yellow handkerchief, and I think had some white spots on it; I do not think there was any red or blue in it - I did not tell Mr. Taylor sooner, because I thought they would beat me.
WILLIAM TAYLOR. My handkerchief was yellow, with white birds' eyes on it; I have not seen it since; this little boy seemed very much frightened.
JOHN LAWS. I am a patrol. The prisoner was delivered to me.
Prisoner's Defence. I had been out of work, and wasgoing to Drury-lane to get some bottles; this gentleman said I had his handkerchief - I said I had not, and this boy said I took it.
GUILTY. Aged 13.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18280529-260]
1339. WILLIAM ALABASTER was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of April, 1 handkerchief, value 1d., the goods of a man unknown, from his person.
GEORGE FREDERICK BOOTH. I am inspector of St. Brides' watch; I was on duty on Ludgate-hill about nine o'clock in the evening, on the 23d of April; I saw the prisoner go into a crowd; I watched him, and saw him take a handkerchief from a person unknown, and put it into his right hand pocket; I seized him, and just at that time there came a sudden rush in the crowd, which took the gentleman away - I took the prisoner to the watch-house; I am a chandler by trade, and have been an officer about twelve months - this is the handkerchief.
Prisoner's Defence. This man takes a false oath: I was going up Ludgate-hill, and saw the handkerchief on the ground - I took it up.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280529-261]
1340. THOMAS ROBERTS was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of May, 13 pairs of unmade trousers, value 3l.6s., and 1 pair of spectacles, value 1s., the goods of John Pearson.
JOHN PEARSON. I am a tailor, and live in Bell-alley, near the Bank; I had thirteen pairs of trousers in my apartment - on the 8th of May at half-past five o'clock in the morning, I went out, and left the prisoner there; he went away, and returned at seven o'clock; he staid there with me until eight o'clock - I then went out, and returned in twenty minutes; the prisoner was gone, and the property likewise; I had left my spectacles on the bed - they were gone, as well as the trousers; I have never got the trousers since - the prisoner was taken on the Friday evening; I charged him with it - he said he knew nothing about it; he is a shoe-maker, and had been at work for my master the day before.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Was any other person there on the day of the robbery? A. No - a person worked there, but he was not there then; I do not know that he had not been there while I was out; I saw the trousers at five o'clock when I made my bed - the other man should have come to work at six; I had seen the prisoner working with spectacles the day before, but he did not borrow mine - I had had no quarrel with him; I was not drunk the day before - the prisoner and the other man were; I took up the other man.
COURT. Q. Who is the other man? A. A man named Joiner; I went to his lodging - the prisoner and he worked together; the prisoner did not return to me.
MARY ANN JAMES. I live where Pearson does. On the morning of the 8th of May, at twenty minutes past eight o'clock, I was at my own door - the prisoner passed by me; he stumbled, and his hat fell off - he took it up and put it on; I saw it was full of something - he also seemed bulky round the waist.
Cross-examined. Q. Is it anything unusual for a tailor to take home work? A. His hat was full - he could hardly put it on.
HENRY AMBROSE. I am an officer. I took the prisoner on Friday evening, and found these spectacles on him - he said he knew nothing of the prosecutor or the property, and was never at the place.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you find any more than one pair of spectacles on him? A. Yes, a pair which he said was his own; he afterwards acknowledged that these belonged to Mr. Pearson, though at first he said he did not know him, and his name was Wilkinson.(Spectacles produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. He allowed me to take the spectacles to work with - I put them into my pocket going out.
GUILTY. Aged 46.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18280529-262]
1341. THOMAS JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of May, 18 yards of ribbon, value 27s., and 14 yards of other ribbon, value 18s., the goods of James Harris.
CHARLES HENRY MARSHALL. I am in the employ of James Harris, haberdasher, of Fore-street, City. On the 28th of May, at half-past eight o'clock in the evening, the prisoner and a boy came into the shop together; I saw the boy draw his hand from a drawer of ribbons on the counter, and give something to the prisoner - I taxed them with it, and before I could get round the counter they ran off, and the prisoner dropped these ribbons against the door: they are my master's property; I followed, and never lost sight of him till the officer took him.
JAMES BUTLER RIDGWAY. I saw the prisoner run out and drop something at the door; I ran on to London-wall, and the officer had got him - Marshall was on before me.
ABRAHAM HAM. I am an officer. I stopped the prisoner - Marshall was pursuing him; these are the ribbons.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I went for three-quarters of a yard of shoe ribbon - the boy came in after me; the witness said, "Have you taken any ribbon" - he said, No; he ran out and dropped the ribbon at my feet.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280529-263]
1342. WILLIAM ELLIOTT was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of May, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of a man unknown, from his person.
CHARLES GEORGE VINCENT. I was a headborough of Clerkenwell. On Friday week I was crossing Fleet-market, and saw the prisoner with another person; the prisoner took a handkerchief from a gentleman's pocket - they crossed the road; I took the prisoner by the collar, and was putting my hand round to his hand - he struck me as I was trying to get him into a pastry-cook's shop; I saw this handkerchief lying at his feet - I sent a boy after the gentleman, but he could not find him; I am in business at No. 23, Bowling-green-street; I have lived in the parish all my life - I do not know who the gentleman was.
Prisoner. Q. Did not you say, if I had not been soresolute you would not have taken me? A. No - I asked how he came to strike me, as he knew me long enough ago; I wished I could have got his partner.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280529-264]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1343. RICHARD CRUTE was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of March, 60 printed bound books, value 20l.; 1 fender, value 5s.; 2 sets of fire-irons, value 5s., and 1 dial clock, value 20s., the goods of William Capon Ayton, his master.
No Evidence. NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280529-265]
Third Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Common Sergeant.
1344. WILLIAM GRIMSHAW & SARAH KING were indicted for feloniously breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Edwin Mellett, on the 16th of April, at Twickenham, and stealing therein 3 spoons, value 1s.; 1 thimble, value 6d.; 1 gold pin, value 5s.; 20 lbs. weight of tea, value 7l.; 50 lbs. weight of bacon, value 30s., and 7 lbs. weight of raisins, value 3s., his property.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
EDWIN MELLETT. I live at Twickenham, and am a grocer. I was the last person up in my house on the 15th of April; it was fastened up securely when I went to bed - at seven o'clock the next morning I came down stairs, and found the door at the bottom of the stair-case bolted on the outside, so as to prevent my getting down; I then went down the other stair-case, and found the door at the bottom of that fastened with a gimblet; I pushed it open and went into the shop, and found the cannister lids lying about - I missed all these articles from the shop and the house; the value of the whole was 10l. or 11l; I went to Mr. Cockburn on the 17th - he produced to me three spoons with my initials filed out in a rough way; I saw the two prisoners in about a week in custody.
JAMES ROBINSON COCKBURN. I am a silversmith, and live at Richmond. King brought these three spoons to me between eleven and twelve o'clock on the morning of the 27th of April, and a thimble, which Mr. Mellett did not claim; the marks on the spoons had been rubbed out; I mentioned that to her - she said she was not aware of any marks ever being on them; she had had them in her possession for twenty years; I said,"That is nonsense" - I did not suppose they had been rubbed out many hours; she said, "You need not be afraid, you know me;" I said, "I know you by sight, and that is all" - I at last gave her 5s. for them; I am confident the erasures could not have been made many hours; Mr. Mellett picked them out of the drawer.
Cross-examined by MR. CRESSWELL. Q. Then you had known her before? A. I had seen her, but did not know her name.
HENRY TODD. I am a constable. I took King about a week after the robbery, at her own house at Hammersmith; Grimshaw lodged there, but he was not at home - he afterwards came and surrendered himself to me; when I took King, she said Grimshaw gave her the spoons; that he was going to work, and saw them laying in the road.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280529-266]
1345. ADAM HALL was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 watch, value 3l.; 2 gold seals, value 1l.,; 1 gold key, value 7s.; 2 jackets, value 1l. 10s.; 4 shirts, value 12s.; 1 handkerchief, value 1s.; 1 waistcoat, value 4s., and 1 pair of trousers, value 8s., the goods of John Lindsay, in his dwelling-house.
JANE LINDSAY. I am the wife of John Lindsay; he is at sea; our house is No. 24, Farmer-street, Shadwell. On the 7th of April I went out at five o'clock in the afternoon, and left the prisoner, who was my lodger, in care of the premises; I left my property all secure in the back parlour - I returned after ten o'clock; I knocked at the door, but could get no admittance; I went to Mr. Jones, the publican, who got in backwards, and let me in; I then missed the property stated - it was worth more than 7l.; I have found a pair of trousers and a shirt at the pawnbroker's; the prisoner was taken on the Monday following.
Cross-examined by MR. J. ALLEY. Q. How long had your husband been gone? A. About six weeks; I had no other lodgers; he is a Greenwich pensioner - I knew I could find him at Mr. Smith's, an agent, in St. Maryaxe; I left a gallon and a half of rum and shrub in my room.
SOPHIA LARDEN. The prisoner brought me this shirt and pair of trousers the beginning of April; I pawned them for him for 4s., and he gave me 4d.; I had not known him before.
Cross-examined. Q. In whose name did you pawn them? A. My own; I do not know the prosecutrix's servant - I saw her at Hicks's-hall, but did not ask her to point out the prisoner, for I knew him: he gave me a glass of gin.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was in liquor, being left with a decanter three parts full of rum - I do not know what I did.
GUILTY. Aged 56.
Of stealing to the value of 99s. only. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280529-267]
1346. HENRY GREENLEY and WILLIAM MEARS were indicted for feloniously breaking and entering the dwelling-house of James Colquhoun, Esq,, on the 24th of April, at St. Clement Danes, and stealing therein 1 hat, value 5s.; 1 pair of boots, value 15s., and 7 pairs of trowsers, value 20s., his property.
MR. JAMES COLQUHOUN. I am a student of law. On the 24th of April I left my chambers in Lincoln's-inn-fields locked, about half past four o'clock - I returned at a quarter past seven o'clock; I found the doors of my sitting-room and bed-room open, and the books and clothes in confusion - I missed my hat from the table, a pair of boots, seven pairs of trowsers, and a great deal of other property; I found a hat, which was not mine, on the table, and the chisel, which I gave the officer - I do not know what parish my chambers are in.
JAMES BROWN. I am a police officer. I took Greenley on the 16th or 17th of May; I found this hat on his head; it has Mr. Colquhoun's address in it - I went and gave him the information; I then went to Mears' lodging, and found these boots.
Cross examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. When was the robbery? A. On the 24th of April - I did not find them till the 16th or 17th of May.
GEORGE GRAVES. I am an officer. I went to search Mears' lodging, and found these boots.
RALPH JOHN MASON. I am a boot-maker. I made these boots for Mr. Colquhoun.
MEARS' Defence. My daughter brought these boots home on the 9th of May; I supposed they belonged to her.
Mears received a good character.
GREENLEY - GUILTY. Aged 21. (See p. 612.)
MEARS - GUILTY. Aged 52.
Of stealing only. - Mears Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280529-268]
1346. JOHN WHITE was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of May, 1 gelding, price 12l., the property of John Moyse.
SIDNEY KERSEY. I am servant to John Moyse: he lives in Little Coram-street - he is servant to Mr. Ellis, of Burton-crescent; I was employed to sell a gelding for Mr. Moyse, on the 16th of May; I got to Smithfield with it about four o'clock - several persons spoke to me about it; one man said, "Is the poney to sell?" I said Yes; he said,"Come out of the market, I have a gentleman here who wishes to see it;" I went with him to St. John's-street, where I saw the prisoner - I rode the poney up and down three times; the prisoner said, "That will do," and asked me the price; I said, "I must have fourteen guineas:" he said, "Will not 12l. do?" I said No; he then said he would give me 13l., and 10s. for myself; I said he should have it, and he asked me to take it to his house in Baltic-street, Old-street: I went there, and saw him - he said, "Give one of the boys in the street the poney to hold; come in and I will pay you;" I left the poney with a boy, and went into the house; I saw the prisoner, and a person he called his sister - he told her to fetch a little ale; she said it was out; he said, "Fetch a pot of porter;" she did, and I drank of it; the prisoner then sent the man who first spoke to me in Smithfield to get a stamp - he did so; the prisoner said, "I am very particular in doing my business - I have it all on stamped paper;" his sister then went out, and took the poney from the boy - the other man went out to her, and said, "You must not take the poney away yet, the bridle does not belong to us;" he then came in, and gave me the bridle: the sister and the other man then went away- the prisoner said to me, "I have no money - I have paid it away for a load of corn." and he put this receipt for me to sign, but I would not; I read it, and thought it was a bill - the prisoner said, "It is a bill - you must take it"; he had told me he was a corn-dealer; when I would not take the bill he asked if I would take corn - I at last took the bill, believing it to be of value; I said I would carry it home, and see if master liked it, and if not I must bring it back again - if master had liked it I should not have interfered further; master sent me back - I went, and saw the prisoner; I said, "Master won't take this, it is not accepted, and not drawn on anybody;" he said he would not part with the poney that night, as he had to go into the country; but his sister has since brought it to the office.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280529-269]
1348. THOMAS FLIGHT was indicted for feloniously breaking and entering the dwelling-house of Isabella Scott, widow, on the 21st of May, at St. Mary, Islington, and stealing therein, 2 muslin dresses, value 15s., the goods of Margaretta Scott, spinster; 3 books, value 10s.; the goods of Elizabeth Davis, spinster; 1 hat, value 8s., and 1 pair of shoes, value 2s., the goods of James Baron; 1 box, value 2s., the goods of the said Isabella Scott; 1 hat, value 6s.; 1 pair of boots, value 4s., and 1 pair of gloves, value 1s., the goods of Edward Bond.
ROBERT BROWN. I am an officer of Islington. On the 21st of May, between three and four o'clock in the morning, I met the prisoner on the path which leads from Colebrook-row to the City-road, about three hundred yards from Mrs. Scott's; I asked what he had got - he made a very impudent answer; his pockets stuck out very much, and I took him to the watch-house - I found on him the articles stated in the indictment; the two gowns were wet - one was in his hat and one in his pocket.
PHOEBE SANDERS. I live with Mrs. Scott, as servant. On Tuesday night, the 20th of May, I fastened up the house and went to bed between ten and eleven o'clock - everything was secure; I got up about half-past six and found the back kitchen window open, a piece cut out of the shutter, and the iron bars which go across, were forced - I know these two dresses - they were wet and were in the back kitchen.
ISABELLA SCOTT. I am a widow and rent this house. I went to bed between eleven and twelve o'clock; this small silver box is mine and was on the front parlour mantel-piece; I know these dresses and other articles - my house is in the parish of St. Mary, Islington; all these things were in the house the day before.
EDWARD BOND. I lodge at the prosecutrix's house. This is my hat; I left it in the hall, with these gloves in it - it was gone in the morning.
Prisoner's Defence. I found the property in the brick field, near the house.
GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 21.
